It's just very frustrating, so I thought I needed to come in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing this foggy feeling and trouble remembering things—days, weeks, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it's been going on for about four months now. It seemed to start right around the time we had some water damage that... well, it pushed us into doing a remodel. So, yes, about four months of this.\n\nDoctor: Since this began around the time of the water damage and remodel, have you also been feeling unusually sad, down, or losing interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Things I used to love, like my watercolors, my tomato plants, line dancing, and watching the cardinals out back, just sit there now and I can't make myself care about them.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been over these past four months—are you having trouble falling asleep, staying asleep, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: My sleep? Well, I haven't really noticed any big changes there. I mean, I go to bed around the same time I always do, and I seem to be sleeping about the same amount. I'm not tossing and turning all night or anything, and I'm not sleeping the day away either. It's just... normal, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other physical changes recently, like feeling unusually cold, gaining weight, having dry skin, or constipation?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My weight has stayed about the same, and I don't feel any colder than usual. No dry skin or stomach issues either. Just the lack of energy and the foggy feeling, really.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually worthless, guilty, or down on yourself these past four months?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I don't feel guilty about anything, and I don't feel worthless. It's really just the lack of energy and not caring about my hobbies like I used to. But I don't feel bad about myself, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious, overwhelmed, or preoccupied specifically about the water damage and remodel, or does the low mood and fogginess feel present all the time regardless of what's happening with the house?\n\nPatient: Well, it's not really about the house. I mean, the remodel is a nuisance, sure, but I don't feel anxious or worried about it. The fogginess and the low energy... they're just there all the time. It doesn't seem to matter what I'm doing or what's going on with the water damage. It's just this constant heavy feeling, no matter what.\n\nDoctor: When you mix up appointment times or forget who you've called, does it feel like you truly can't recall the information afterward, or more like you have trouble focusing long enough to take it in in the first place?\n\nPatient: I think it's more that my head just feels foggy. That's really what brought me in, actually. I just keep mixing up simple things. Like... I'll get appointment times wrong, or I'll mess up the dates for the art exhibits, or I'll forget which volunteer I already called. It's not that I wasn't paying attention, it's just like the information gets all jumbled up in my head because of this fog.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back over these four months, did the foggy, forgetful feeling start before the low mood and loss of interest in your hobbies, or did they both begin around the same time?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure which came first. It seems like they both just sort of crept up on me around the same time. I didn't notice the fogginess before the low energy or anything like that... it feels like it all just kind of rolled in together over these last few months.\n\nDoctor: In the past four months, have you started any new medications or supplements, had any illnesses, infections, or a head injury, or increased your use of alcohol or other substances?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't started taking anything new, no new pills or vitamins. I haven't been sick, and I definitely haven't hit my head. And I don't really drink much anyway, so that hasn't changed either. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, yes, it really has. I'm not managing things on my own very well right now. 

I’ve actually missed some doctor visits recently, and... well, I tangled up my schedule for the senior center so badly that my husband has had to step in and take over. He's been helping me with making phone calls and keeping the calendar straight. While depressive symptoms (anergia, anhedonia) are present, the failure to benefit from cueing on memory testing argues against pseudodementia from depression and instead indicates a true amnestic/cognitive disorder.
